```text
Interesting results from the 4-method κ analysis. Key findings:

1. **κ drops from 0.187 to 0.167** when removing NaiveXCor — it does NOT improve, contradicting the naive expectation.

2. **Why?** At α=1.0, NaiveXCor (81.2%) actually agrees with PermG and DiffXCorr (both 75%). The low κ is driven by Threshold (0%) and TY (50%) disagreeing with the PermG/DiffXCorr cluster. 

Removing NaiveXCor eliminates a method that was actually aligned with the majority, which explains the κ degradation.

3. **Voting comparison**:
   - K≥3/5 (paper): FPR 5.0%, TPR 20.8-29.2%
   - K≥2/4: FPR 17.5%, TPR 29.2-54.2% — too high FPR
   - K≥3/4: FPR 2.5%, TPR 4.2-8.3% — too low TPR

4. **Critical observation**: The null FPR data uses 40 pairs (5 seeds × 8 pairs), but the paper reports 60 pairs (10 seeds × 6 pairs using D102 basis). This is a fundamentally different analysis setup. The paper's K≥3/5 FPR of 10.0% (6/60) doesn't match this analysis's 5.0% (2/40), and I'm realizing the paper uses a D102 6-pair basis that excludes certain methods.

There's also a context mismatch: the paper's κ=0.185 comes from GPT-2M at α=1.0 with just 2 seeds, while this analysis uses Pythia-410M across different α values. The core discrepancy likely stems from the different seed counts (5 vs 10) and pair bases (8 vs 6), which I need to flag as a significant issue. pairs / 10 seeds basis, and there's a false positive rate discrepancy between 5.0% and 10.0% that I need to resolve before this can be included in the paper. Let me draft my review now.
```

**Metric trajectories (T=11, gen_0 through gen_10):**


Token entropy drops sharply from gen_0 to gen_1 (3.048 → 2.435), then plateaus and slightly increases through gen_10. Distinct-1 shows a different pattern—it ticks up initially but then declines steadily from gen_2 onward, ending at 0.0653, a 36.6% drop from the starting point.

Now I'm wondering if PermG will catch this unusual entropy trajectory where it dips then recovers, since that's different from the monotonic decline I'd expect. Looking at the first differences, entropy mostly rebounds after gen_1 with small positive changes, while diversity keeps dropping—this entropy-then-recovery pattern combined with continuous diversity loss is what I need to check for. I need to run the D102 analysis pipeline to get the actual PermG p-values rather than relying on raw metrics alone. I'm also comparing OPT-1.3B's α=0.50 behavior against Pythia-410M to validate whether the pattern holds across architectures, and noting that the 11 data points (gen_0 through gen_10) versus the expected 10 might actually give PermG better statistical power for detecting relationships.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
EXIT_CODE=0 ✓. The CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES was set in the tmux command (I saw it earlier in ps output: `export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0`), so it's confirmed from the process listing.

Now let me analyze the metrics and send my audit report. Key observations:

**OPT-1.3B α=0.50 s42 metrics (T=11):**

| Gen | Entropy | Distinct-1 | Distinct-2 | PPL |
|-----|---------|-----------|-----------|-----|
| 0 | 3.048 | 0.1030 | 0.5255 | 24.88 |


| 1 | 2.435 | 0.1035 | 0.5260 | 17.75 |
| 2 | 2.459 | 0.0856 | 0.4447 | 19.75 |
| 3 | 2.493 | 0.0781 | 0.3955 | 20.55 |
| 4 | 2.529 | 0.0734 | 0.3550 | 21.37 |
| 5 | 2.546 | 0.0705 | 0.3333 | 22.41 |
| 6 | 2.549 | 0.0685 | 0.3115 |

Generating metrics table... Looking at the distinct-1 and distinct-2 metrics, they both show a similar pattern — staying relatively flat between generations 0 and 1, then declining steadily through generation 10. The perplexity metric is interesting too: it drops sharply at generation 1 but then gradually climbs back toward its original value. What's striking is how differently entropy behaves at α=0.50 compared to α=1.0 — at the higher alpha value it declines monotonically, but at 0.50 it crashes initially then partially recovers, which creates complications for the first-differenced entropy calculations.

Looking at the lagged correlations between entropy and distinct-1 changes, there's a clear temporal offset: entropy drops first at generation 0→1, while distinct-1 doesn't start declining until generation 1→2. But after that initial lead, the two metrics move in opposite directions — entropy climbing while distinct-1 continues falling — which makes the relationship harder to pin down.

This opposite-direction dynamic is actually revealing something important about what happens at intermediate contamination levels. The entropy shock still occurs like it does at full contamination, but then it partially rebounds because the 50% real data keeps feeding diversity into the system. Meanwhile, distinct-1 stays suppressed because the model has already locked onto its dominant modes and doesn't recover that representational breadth. For PermG detection purposes, this non-monotonic entropy pattern could be problematic — the lead relationship between entropy and distinct-1 at generation 1 is real, but those opposing trajectories afterward might obscure the signal in the differenced series. I should wait for the D102 analysis to see whether this actually shows up as detectable.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the D102 analysis results for C2-s42 (OPT-1.3B α=0.50). Let me analyze:

**PermG results (the primary detector):**
- Forward: **0/8** FDR-significant pairs
- Reverse: **0/8** FDR-significant pairs
- Key pair token_entropy→distinct_1: p_fdr = 0.112 (borderline, not significant)

**Per-method breakdown:**
- NaiveXCorr: Not reported separately in summary but token_entropy→distinct_1 has peak_lag=5 (positive, meaning reverse direction by D111 convention... 

Wait, I need to reconsider the lag interpretation. A peak_lag=5 means entropy at time t correlates with distinct_1 at time t+5, so entropy is leading by five steps. The direction filter in D111 marks this as reverse causality since it's looking for peak_lag<0 to indicate forward direction. This positive lag suggests entropy is the leading indicator, which would be reverse in the filtering scheme.

Looking at the actual consensus results, naive_xcorr isn't contributing forward votes for token_entropy→distinct_1. The two forward votes come from diff_xcorr and ty_granger instead. Examining the broader pattern across methods: PermG shows no detection at all, DiffXCorr finds bidirectional relationships at both significance levels, the threshold method detects nothing, TY barely registers with one case, and no pairs reach the three-out-of-five agreement threshold. The PermG result for token_entropy→distinct_1 is striking though—it's producing an F-statistic of 375.66, which is substantial despite not passing the forward detection criteria.

The permutation p-value of 0.084 sits just above the significance threshold, and after multiple testing correction it becomes 0.112, so it doesn't qualify as significant. The high F-statistic isn't unusual under the permutation null, likely because with only about nine effective observations after first-differencing, the permutation test becomes quite conservative. This borderline case highlights how the cross-architecture validation at α=0.50 fails to confirm the phase transition boundary—OPT-1.3B shows zero significant PermG forward pairs at this intermediate alpha level, suggesting the model remains in a detection blind zone at this setting.

The negative result for R16-W5 is notable because the paper currently restricts cross-architecture findings to α=1.0, and this analysis shows that extending to α=0.50 doesn't generalize the transition boundary across architectures. I'm noticing the analysis actually includes four downstreams rather than three, which means it's working with eight pairs total instead of the six pairs used in D102, so I need to verify which script version was actually executed. pair basis (which excludes mauve).

This is a methodological issue — I need to flag this. The D102 standard excludes mauve, so the paper should use D102 6-pair basis for consistency.

But even excluding the 2 mauve pairs, PermG is still 0/6 significant. The non-mauve pairs show no significant forward detection across token_entropy, ece, and distinct metrics.

So OPT-1.3B at α=0.50 shows 0/6 (D102) or 0/8 (full) PermG forward detection — this is the negative scenario I flagged earlier. But I should consider whether this is actually unexpected or if different model architectures have different contamination thresholds. Pythia-410M shows strong detection at this level, while OPT-1.3B might have its transition boundary elsewhere, and the paper's main claims focus on Pythia anyway. I'm ready to finalize and send this analysis to the authors.

R16-W5（cross-architecture intermediate-α validation）**未获正面结果**——不能说 \"transition generalizes across architectures\"\n3. 但这**不矛盾**现有论文——论文已说 \"cross-architecture evaluation is limited to α=1.0\" 和 \"the phase diagram is Pythia-family-only\"\n\n### 科学解释（支持 non-detection）\n\nEntropy 非单调性是根本原因。OPT-1.3B α=0.50 的 entropy 先降后升（3.048→2.435→2.631），而 Pythia-410M α=0.50 entropy 单调下降。First-differencing 后：\n- Δentropy: [−0.614, +0.025, +0.034, +0.035, ...] — 仅 1 个负值\n- Δdistinct_1: [+0.001, −0.018, −0.008, ...] — 持续负值\n\nGen_1 之后 entropy 和 diversity 方向相反，PermG 无法检测到 forward Granger precedence。这是 α=0.50 处 50% real data 部分恢复 entropy 的物理结果，不是方法缺陷。\n\n### 建议处理方式\n\n**不需要加载 negative-framing-review skill**——这不是对核心 claim 的否定，而是对 generalization scope 的进一步确认。\n\n论文修改建议：\n1. §4.5 增加 1-2 句：*\"At intermediate contamination (α=0.50), OPT-1.3B yields 0/6 PermG-significant pairs (strongest p_fdr=0.112), suggesting the detection boundary α_c is architecture-dependent—OPT-1.3B requires higher contamination for detection onset.\"*\n2. 这实际上**强化**了 limitations.tex 中 \"phase diagram is Pythia-family-only\" 的诚实性\n3. 不要把这包装成 \"重要发现\"——它是 expected negative，简单报告即可\n\n### 下一步\n\n- C2-s43 (OPT-1.3B α=0.50 seed 43) 是否仍需运行？如果目标是验证 OPT-1.3B 的 α_c 位置，一个 negative result at α=0.50 已经提供了信息。s43 能确认 seed 一致性，但不会改变 PermG=0/8 的定性结论（除非 seed variance 非常大）。由 Director 决定。",
  "project": "distrib_canary_collapse"
}

**必须覆盖以下 5 个 section（缺一不可，无内容写 N/A）**:

  本项目特定的不可违反约束（GPU 设备号、服务器名、账号、路径限制等）。
  写本项目特定的，不要抄 CLAUDE.md 通用 L1 红线。
  下一个"你"第一眼看到的就是这里，写错了会被 Director CRITICAL。

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
  - 服务器名 + 工作目录 + Python 路径 + conda env
  - 模型路径 / 数据集路径 / wandb config / HF cache
  - 活跃进程：PID + tmux session + cron 任务 ID
  写一次就稳定，下一个"你"不用再 SSH 探查。

### 3. 📊 当前状态
  - 你刚做完什么 / 正在做什么
  - registry 里 RUNNING/PREPARING 的实验列表 + 一句话摘要
  - 等待中的 Worker 回调 / Cron / Director 决策

### 4. 📋 待办（条件触发式）
  格式 "条件 X 满足 → 动作 Y"。例如:
  - "Falcon-H1R 下载完成 → sed 修 MODEL_PATH → dry-run → 提交"
  - "pilot_011 出 GC4 结果 → ≥2pp 转架构 narrative，<2pp 写 null-result"

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
  - 项目核心结论 / 决策链（D001..）
  - 下一个"你"最容易判断错的事 / 容易踩的坑

**写作原则**：
- 第 1 节必须是项目特定的（不是通用 L1 红线）
- 第 2 节让下一轮 BOOT 后能立即开工，避免重新 SSH 探查
